### 2026-10-07 — (resolved locally; replacement exact gate required) One-hour owners killed a healthy complete gate

- **What happened**: The non-short-circuit gate passed every preflight and the first four of eight serial shards, retained two presentation-contract failures, and continued into `unit-2:batch-018`. At roughly one hour, the background ownership boundary terminated the healthy process tree before the remaining shards could report, while `.gate-status` still said `RUNNING` for a dead PID.
- **Root cause**: Both `GATE_TIMEOUT` and the independent agent-watchdog ceiling defaulted to 3,600 seconds even though a cold 244-batch release gate legitimately exceeds one hour. The status readers also collapsed a dead `RUNNING` receipt into `UNKNOWN` or printed the contradictory phrase `FINISHED: RUNNING`.
- **Fix applied**: The bounded launcher and watchdog now share a six-hour cold-gate ceiling, with the watchdog message rendering the configured value instead of a hard-coded `>1h`. `active-work-status` distinguishes `RUNNING`, `ORPHANED`, and `TIMEOUT`, and `gate-status-check` reports a dead `RUNNING` owner as an aborted orphan rather than a normal finish.
- **Evidence**: Failing-first tests reproduced both one-hour defaults, the orphaned JSON state, and the contradictory Make status surface. The repaired focused timeout/status cases pass, while the interrupted gate's immutable log remains evidence of the two already-repaired 60-to-61 slide-count assertions and of continued progress through shard 5/8 before termination. Full focused replay and a replacement exact gate remain required.
- **Practitioner evidence**: [pytest-xdist #220](https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest-xdist/issues/220) has tracked the need for a controller-owned session timeout since 2017, while [pytest-xdist #61](https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest-xdist/issues/61) records workers that can remain waiting before tests execute. [pytest-timeout #60](https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest-timeout/issues/60) separately distinguishes per-test limits from a whole-session deadline. These reports support retaining a generous outer safety bound while continuing to enforce narrow per-test, quiet-output, and process-group deadlines.
- **ZDD and rollback**: This changes only validation ownership and reporting. It adds no worker, service, listener, schema, or deployment mutation; per-test and quiet-output bounds remain unchanged. Rollback waits for any active gate to reach terminal state, then restores the former defaults without touching application traffic.
- **Lesson**: A safety deadline must exceed the valid worst-case operation it owns. Killing healthy exhaustive evidence at the deadline is candidate churn, not protection.

### 2026-10-07 — (resolved locally; exact-candidate gate required) Presentation honesty validation ran silently for over a minute

- **What happened**: The real `deck-honesty` target spent more than 60 seconds collecting the repository's tests without emitting output. The command eventually passed, but operators could not distinguish progress from a hung process.
- **Root cause**: The target called `build_deck.py --check` directly instead of the repository's shared observed-command boundary, even though test discovery makes the checker a long-running operation.
- **Fix applied**: `deck-honesty` now delegates to `run-watched` with an explicit label and inherited heartbeat, quiet, maximum-runtime, retention, and log-root bounds. It preserves the checker exit status and adds no retry.
- **Evidence**: The structural observability regression failed first against the direct invocation and now passes. The real behavioral example emitted start, 30-second and 60-second heartbeats, then a passing terminal result at 65.3 seconds. Make validation reports 1,111 targets with zero duplicates and the 196-target contract passes.
- **Practitioner evidence**: [pytest-timeout #60](https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest-timeout/issues/60) distinguishes a whole-session deadline from per-test timeouts; [pytest-timeout #159](https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest-timeout/issues/159) records orphaned child work after timeout. Both support an observable owner with bounded cleanup rather than a silent direct child.
- **ZDD and rollback**: The wrapper changes only local validation observability. It opens no listener, changes no application state, and writes only bounded logs under the existing owned gate-log root. Rollback is source-only after any active observed invocation reaches terminal state.
- **Lesson**: A command is operationally broken when its progress is invisible, even if it eventually exits successfully.

### 2026-10-07 — (resolved locally; exact-candidate gate required) Commit preflight could publish another worker's staged files

- **What happened**: Two scoped commits began with the intended files staged, ran the long collection preflight, and then included unrelated files that another worker staged before `git commit`. Separately, the gate lock stored a process start time but treated any live matching PID as its owner, so PID reuse or a zombie could indefinitely block history mutation.
- **Root cause**: `ship-commit` validated only the staged path list before collection and did not bind the publication step to an immutable index tree. `ship-commit-files` also did not pass its requested file set into that validation. Gate ownership checked PID liveness but ignored the already-recorded start identity and process state.
- **Fix applied**: Scoped commits now require the exact requested staged paths, snapshot `git write-tree` before collection, and refuse publication when the tree changes. Gate ownership now compares the live process start token, treats zombies as dead, and reports process state in live-owner diagnostics.
- **Evidence**: Failing-first regressions reproduced exact-scope drift, post-collection index drift, PID reuse, zombie ownership, and missing process-state diagnostics. The repaired gate/commit-lock suite passes 63/63; Ruff, mypy across 1,464 source files, Make validation, the 196-target contract, and duplicate-target checks are green. An exact-candidate gate remains required after commit and integration.
- **Practitioner evidence**: [OpenAI Codex #17482](https://github.com/openai/codex/issues/17482) reports parallel agent Git mutations colliding on `index.lock`; [git-ai #1240](https://github.com/git-ai-project/git-ai/issues/1240) reports background checkpoints racing interactive Git and leaving stale locks; [Paseo #1304](https://github.com/getpaseo/paseo/issues/1304) reproduces a recycled PID being mistaken for a daemon and recommends start-time identity validation.
- **ZDD and rollback**: No service, schema, listener, credential, or deployment changes. The guard rejects only ambiguous repository mutations and reclaims only proven stale ownership. Rollback is source-only after any active gate reaches terminal evidence.
- **Lesson**: A lock identifies an owner incarnation, not merely a numeric PID, and a scoped commit remains scoped only if its exact index tree is revalidated at the publication boundary.

### 2026-10-07 — (resolved locally; exact-candidate gate required) Disk preflight could not reclaim downloaded gate artifacts

- **What happened**: The combined candidate passed its eight focused regressions, Node compatibility, plugin E2E, and collection, but the exact gate failed immediately at 91% filesystem use against the unchanged 90% ceiling. Automatic cleanup safely removed completed worktrees and 616 MiB of uv cache yet ignored 245 MiB of reproducible `.gate-logs/ci-artifacts`, so a retry could only fail the same admission check.
- **Root cause**: The generic cache manager allowed user-cache roots but not the repository's generated gate-artifact root. The disk preflight could classify the pressure but had no exact, validate-first operator path for reclaiming a downloaded CI artifact tree.
- **Fix applied**: The cache manager now permits only an existing non-symlink immediate child of this checkout's canonical `.gate-logs`. Existing exact-child, sibling-preservation, path-escape, and symlink checks remain unchanged; validation remains the default and apply must be explicit.
- **Evidence**: The new regression failed first with `root is not allowlisted for removal`, then passed after the narrow allowlist change. The complete 40-test cache/lifecycle slice is green; the subprocess-aware profile reports 97% branch-aware coverage with its measured file above 75%; the real validate-first command preserved the candidate, explicit apply removed exactly `.gate-logs/ci-artifacts`, and the unchanged disk preflight moved from 91% failure to 90% PASS. The combined presentation preview then exposed one stale pre-split source range; a new no-browser regression failed first on that exact path/range, and now passes against the corrected facade/storage links. Chromium passes 17/17 plus compact containment. Two isolated WebKit replays now deterministically stall in Playwright's session-scoped browser launch and time out at 180 seconds even after the exact pinned installer succeeds; no chart assertion starts. A subsequent full mypy replay exposed one NumPy pooled-mean `Any` return missed by scoped validation; real `float64` ndarray normalization repairs it, the 146-test video slice is green, and all 1,464 source files now type-check. The first replacement gate then failed closed because the two cohesive durable decision-state modules were absent from the static split-module mapping. Its existing exact-mapping regression failed first, the facade/inheritance chain is now pinned without an allowlist, 47 focused tests pass, and the audit reports 1,310/1,310 modules covered. The restarted exact gate remains required; native Safari and Playwright WebKit remain honestly unclaimed on this host.
- **Practitioner evidence**: [GitHub Community #205363](https://github.com/orgs/community/discussions/205363) reports retained self-hosted runner workspaces and recommends explicit end-of-run cleanup over a time sweep that can catch active work. [GitHub Community #179725](https://github.com/orgs/community/discussions/179725) records runner disk exhaustion from accumulated build layers. [Playwright #41870](https://github.com/microsoft/playwright/issues/41870) reproduces the current macOS 26.5 bundled-WebKit deadlock, reports that forced reinstall does not repair it, and confirms native Safari remains independent.
- **ZDD and rollback**: No service or active process is stopped. Remote CI remains authoritative and exact local downloads are regenerable. Rollback removes the repository gate-log root from the allowlist; already reclaimed copies need no migration.
- **Lesson**: A disk guard must pair a hard ceiling with an ownership-safe recovery path for every large generated class it reports, or it converts recoverable pressure into a deterministic retry loop.
### 2026-10-07 — (resolved locally; exact-candidate gate required) Terminal promotion inherited diagnostic readiness bypasses

- **What happened**: S83.166 correctly excludes its own terminal publication action from the pre-publication task cycle, but `release-promote` called `release-readiness` without setting either diagnostic bypass. An ambient or command-line `RELEASE_ALLOW_INCOMPLETE_TASKS=1` or `RELEASE_ALLOW_INVALID_RECEIPT=1` could therefore skip predecessor or reviewed-head admission immediately before validation or the development-to-master fast-forward. No release bypass is claimed to have occurred; the structural gap was found while advancing the still-open release action.
- **Root cause**: The readiness checker intentionally reads two environment variables for isolated diagnostic workflows. GNU Make exports environment-originated variables to recipe commands and propagates command-line assignments through recursive Make, while the destructive target treated omission as equivalent to false. Diagnostic authority and terminal release authority therefore shared an unsealed process environment.
- **Fix applied**: Both validation and live `release-promote` readiness delegations now pass `RELEASE_ALLOW_INCOMPLETE_TASKS=0` and `RELEASE_ALLOW_INVALID_RECEIPT=0` explicitly. Direct `release-readiness` invocations retain their documented diagnostic overrides; S83.166 remains excluded only from its unavoidable self-cycle.
- **Evidence**: The composed-Makefile regression failed first 1/1 on the validation call, then passed unchanged 1/1 after the two terminal call sites were repaired. The selected admission/promotion/readiness replay passes 91/91 under warnings-as-errors; branch-aware coverage is 97% for the measured readiness checker, above 85% aggregate and 75% per-file. Scoped Ruff and strict mypy, Markdown, Make validation, the 196-target contract, task ledger/integrity, and all 237 resource-ownership entries are green. Collection reports 119,693/119,711 tests with 18 intentional deselections and zero errors. The complete exact-candidate gate remains required.
- **Practitioner evidence**: GNU Make's [recursive-variable contract](https://www.gnu.org/software/make/manual/html_node/Variables_002fRecursion.html) documents environment export and command-line propagation through `MAKEFLAGS`. [au-ts/sddf issue #337](https://github.com/au-ts/sddf/issues/337) reproduces divergent recursive override behavior on GNU Make 3.81 and 4.4.1 and resolves it with explicit sub-Make values. The long-lived [actions/runner issue #789](https://github.com/actions/runner/issues/789) records stale environment values surviving repeated composite-action use, while GitHub's [deployment-environment contract](https://docs.github.com/en/actions/concepts/workflows-and-actions/deployment-environments) requires every protection rule to pass before a deployment job starts.
- **ZDD and rollback**: The repair is a pre-mutation scalar policy boundary. It adds no process, poll, network call, worktree, cache, schema, credential, daemon restart, or paid resource; failures remain observable through readiness JSON before any ref, tag, or artifact change. Before publication rollback is a source revert. After publication, history and receipts remain immutable and any incompatibility uses the existing fix-forward release path.
- **Lesson**: A diagnostic bypass must be explicitly disabled at every destructive delegation. Omission is not a fail-closed value when the build system inherits caller state.
### 2026-10-07 — (resolved locally; exact-candidate gate required) Live-session compute fence blocked providerless dispatch

- **What happened**: The first live-session fence correctly stopped provider reconciliation from an uncommitted caller-owned `AsyncSession`, but it also marked compute unavailable when no compute lifecycle existed. Two project-isolation integration tests then claimed work successfully yet emitted no HTTP job. The compatibility path still ran all phases in one transaction, so simply bypassing the provider check would have published a job before its claim committed. An initial lifecycle split also bypassed the patchable `_run_phases` seam and made the concurrent-tick regression wait until its 180-second timeout.
- **Root cause**: The effect boundary conflated an attached session with an attempted provider effect, while the caller-owned-session orchestration lacked an explicit claim commit. The first repair placed the split in `_tick_once` instead of the established serialized phase-runner seam.
- **Fix applied**: The compatibility `_run_phases` path now commits the claim and execution leases before compute or dispatch while leaving the caller-owned session open. Compute capability is resolved before the active-session provider fence: an absent or playbook-only runner is `externally_managed` and dispatch-ready without provisioning, while a runner with `reconcile_execution_environment` still receives `claim_transaction_open`, zero provider calls, and zero dispatch. Keeping the split inside `_run_phases` preserves tick-lock serialization and existing instrumentation.
- **Evidence**: Four failing-first lifecycle/admission regressions reproduced the missing commit, providerless rejection, and absent admission phase; the broader run then preserved the concurrency timeout rather than hiding it. The repaired affected slice passes 56/56, including both original integration nodes. The warning-strict branch-aware profile passes 504 tests with one skip at 93% aggregate coverage: `compute_lifecycle.py` is 97% and `tick_lifecycle.py` is 89%, both above the 75% file floor. Ruff, strict mypy, Markdown lint, conflict scan, the 198-target Make contract, and its validate-only example are green. Collection reports 119,778/119,796 tests with 18 intentional deselections and zero errors.
- **Practitioner evidence**: Sidekiq issue [#5239](https://github.com/sidekiq/sidekiq/issues/5239) describes pre-commit job publication as a problem present “since Day One.” In discussion [#5725](https://github.com/sidekiq/sidekiq/discussions/5725), an operator reports enabling transactional push after more than 237 million processed jobs without an observed issue. Celery's maintained [task guide](https://github.com/celery/celery/blob/main/docs/userguide/tasks.rst) documents the same worker-before-commit race and its `delay_on_commit` boundary. `docs/features/DURABLE_CLAIM_COMPUTE_FENCE.md` applies that established commit boundary without inventing a compute lifecycle for compatibility runners.
- **ZDD and rollback**: The compatibility commit occurs before job publication and starts no new listener, process, thread, provider request, or cloud resource. The caller still owns session closure; providerless dispatch uses the existing bounded sequential path. Provider-managed work remains fail-closed until the session-factory path releases its claim session. Rollback is a source revert only after proving no active caller depends on the fence; provider creation is never a rollback action.
- **Lesson**: Fence the effect that actually exists. Durable claims must commit before any dispatch, but an attached reusable session cannot justify blocking work when the composition has no compute-provider effect to perform.
### 2026-10-07 — (resolved locally; live receipt and exact-candidate gate required) Paid Azure proof trusted prose-only Environment protection

- **What happened**: The hosted Azure Container Apps workflow named `azure-containerapp-live`, but nothing inspected the Environment before minting its OIDC assertion. Required reviewers, self-review prevention, administrator bypass, and deployment-ref restrictions could drift while the YAML and hermetic tests remained green.
- **Root cause**: The documentation described a protected Environment while the executable boundary validated only workflow syntax and credential shape. GitHub stores protection rules outside the repository, and the workflow had no read-only reconciliation step between Environment admission and Azure identity creation.
- **Fix applied**: A dependency-free validator and GitHub CLI adapter make exactly two bounded `GET` requests before assertion minting. They require disabled administrator bypass, one nonempty required-reviewer rule with self-review prevention, custom policies with a complete response, and the exact `development`, `master`, and `v*` patterns. Nonzero responses, timeouts, and process-launch errors collapse to bounded rejection codes; success output contains no reviewer identity or API payload. The workflow token gains only `actions: read`, and the adapter has no mutation verb.
- **Evidence**: Failing-first tests showed the workflow guard absent and `can_admins_bypass=true` accepted. A fresh-runner behavioral replay then exposed and removed an eager `infra` dependency before proof; a follow-up failing-first timeout regression exposed an uncensored subprocess exception. Focused workflow/guard tests pass 31/31; branch-aware coverage is 95% across both new files, the validator reports 100%, and neither file is below 75%. The real network-free make example, 197-target contract, scoped Ruff and strict mypy, and feature Markdown are green. A live Environment receipt and exact-candidate gate remain required.
- **Practitioner evidence**: GitHub Community [discussion 12241](https://github.com/orgs/community/discussions/12241) records the long-running self-approval gap; [discussion 39054](https://github.com/orgs/community/discussions/39054) shows stale Environment branch names blocking deployment after a rename; and [discussion 141195](https://github.com/orgs/community/discussions/141195) reports unexpected ref selection in chained workflows. [go-github issue 2722](https://github.com/google/go-github/issues/2722) records that the live API exposed `can_admins_bypass` before the REST schema documented it, so omission is rejected instead of treated as safe.
- **ZDD and rollback**: The new step is read-only and runs before assertion creation or paid compute, so rejection creates nothing to tear down and does not interrupt ordinary CI or application traffic. Configure the Environment before rollout. If GitHub changes its response, retain the stricter Environment, repair the adapter, and rerun; source rollback does not require Azure cleanup or a service restart.
- **Lesson**: A protected deployment boundary is external mutable state. Naming it in YAML is not evidence; reconcile its exact live protection before creating the identity that can spend money.

### 2026-10-06 — (resolved locally; exact-candidate gate required) Catalog-truth refs were caller-mutable

- **What happened**: Hosted `unit-3b` failed `test_catalog_truth_make_target_is_pinned_and_safe_by_default` because the catalog-truth recipe delegated through `$(SELF_IMPROVE_CATALOG_TRUTH_*_REF)` instead of exposing its two immutable SHAs. A behavioral reproduction then supplied alternate 40-character refs on the Make command line; the real validate-only target accepted them and failed while resolving the substituted Git objects.
- **Root cause**: Ordinary Makefile assignments do not override command-line assignments, and recursive Make propagates command-line variables. The wrapper therefore described a fixed sentinel while leaving its comparison identity replaceable at invocation time.
- **Fix applied**: The recursive benchmark call now passes both pinned SHAs literally, and the split entrypoint exposes no caller-mutable catalog aliases. The regression invokes the real safe target with hostile public and legacy-alias values and requires the rendered plan to retain both canonical refs.
- **Evidence**: The hosted structural case, the executable override case, and the missing narrow coverage contract all failed before repair. The complete catalog-truth fixture replay passes 8/8 under warnings-as-errors, the fixture plus adjacent Azure contract replay passes 12/12, and the tracked subprocess-aware coverage slice reports 100% for the compatibility entry point with its sole measured file above the 75% floor. The broader canonical replay completed 7,398 passed, five skipped, and one expected failure, then truthfully failed its unchanged audit on separately owned scheduler debt: `event_loop/loop.py` branch coverage was 71.8% and aggregate branch coverage was 84.7%. That debt was neither waived nor changed here. Scoped Ruff and mypy, Markdown, Make validation, the 188-target contract, the real behavioral example, and task integrity are green; full collection finds 117,975 of 117,976 tests with one intentional deselection and no errors. Commit evidence remains required.
- **Practitioner evidence**: GNU Make's [target-specific variable contract](https://www.gnu.org/software/make/manual/html_node/Target_002dspecific.html) defines command-line precedence and the `override` exception. A long-running [Stack Overflow report](https://stackoverflow.com/questions/32087102/command-line-variables-in-makefile-expand-to-weird-values) reproduces command-line replacement of target-specific values, and [au-ts/sddf issue #337](https://github.com/au-ts/sddf/issues/337) records recursive propagation differences across GNU Make 3.81 and 4.4.1.
- **ZDD and rollback**: This is a Make delegation change with no listener, schema, credential, deployment, or daemon mutation. Validate-only allocates no model or candidate worktree; a live run retains the refs resolved at admission and follows existing isolated-worktree teardown. Rollback is an ordinary source revert and does not interrupt application traffic.
- **Lesson**: A reproducibility pin must be immutable at the final delegation boundary. A descriptive constant is insufficient when the build tool gives callers higher precedence.

### 2026-10-05 — (resolved locally; exact-candidate gate required) Interrupted async gate retained a false `RUNNING` receipt

- **What happened**: `scripts/gate_async.sh` atomically published `RUNNING <epoch> <pid>`, but direct `SIGINT` or `SIGTERM` termination stopped the wrapper before its ordinary PASS/FAIL epilogue. Readers then saw a permanently active gate even though the owner was gone, and the portable PID-file lock remained until a later launcher diagnosed it as stale.
- **Root cause**: The async wrapper treated only its child command's return as a terminal transition. It had no EXIT/INT/TERM lifecycle handler, so signal exit codes, terminal status publication, descendant cleanup, and owned-lock release were unrelated best-effort operations rather than one idempotent boundary. Its default child was also `run_gate.sh`, the pytest phase alone, so even a local trap could not attest the preflight, lint, typecheck, collection, and smoke phases of the complete gate.
- **Fix applied**: The wrapper now owns the non-recursive complete `make gate` boundary while every runtime test injects a stub command. The existing temp-file-plus-rename writer serves normal, abnormal, and signal exits. INT and TERM publish `ABORTED` with preserved 130/143 evidence, delegate verified descendant teardown to `kill_owned_gate.py`, and terminate the exact child during the pre-lock admission window. Unexpected post-RUNNING exits and ordinary child signal death publish `FAILED` evidence with the exact 128-plus-signal code. Cleanup masks repeat termination, removes only the current PID's async-lock record, and preserves complete gate PASSED or FAILED receipts byte-for-byte so a late signal or wrapper epilogue cannot rewrite authoritative evidence.
- **Evidence**: The failing-first signal regressions returned `-2`/`-15` and left `RUNNING`; pivot regressions then proved the pytest-only default, full-receipt overwrite, and parent-only orphan. The repaired focused suite passes 16/16 across the no-recursion whole-gate boundary, PASS/FAIL fallback, full PASSED/FAILED preservation, child `SIGKILL` (`rc=137`), wrapper INT (`rc=130`), wrapper TERM (`rc=143`), atomic terminal replacement, descendant and lock release, concurrent refusal, and late-signal PASS preservation. The expanded gate/kill/shell/observability replay passes 262/262, the shell-quality replay passes 136/136, collection finds 117,805 of 117,806 tests with one intentional deselection and no errors, and scoped Ruff, strict test mypy, and Markdown lint are green. Bash is outside the Python line-coverage instrument; the process suite directly executes every required changed terminal state rather than fabricating a Python percentage. Exact-candidate gate evidence remains required.
- **Practitioner evidence**: The long-lived Unix & Linux reports [Keep exit codes when trapping SIGINT and similar?](https://unix.stackexchange.com/questions/235582/keep-exit-codes-when-trapping-sigint-and-similar) and [“trap ... INT TERM EXIT” really necessary?](https://unix.stackexchange.com/questions/57940/trap-int-term-exit-really-necessary) document the durable failure modes: signal handlers otherwise change or lose 128-plus-signal status, EXIT plus signal traps can run cleanup twice, and repeated interrupts can cut cleanup short. The repair therefore uses explicit 130/143 handlers, disables re-entry during publication, and leaves one fail-closed EXIT fallback.
- **ZDD and rollback**: This is a launcher control-plane change with no listener, schema, credential, daemon, or application restart. New invocations adopt the terminal-state contract immediately; an already-running old launcher is allowed to finish before replacement. Rollback likewise waits for terminal evidence and released ownership, then reverts the wrapper without interrupting application traffic.
- **Lesson**: Publishing `RUNNING` creates an obligation: every catchable termination path must atomically replace it with terminal evidence before relinquishing ownership, while cleanup must never rewrite success.

### 2026-10-05 — (resolved locally; replacement exact gate required) Combined candidate exposed unowned quality and repair-selector drift

- **What happened**: The first combined development head passed its 218 affected gate-lifecycle tests, then the CI-ledger evidence replay exposed two additional deterministic failures before push: 222 source files were below the unchanged maintainability-index floor budget of 220, and the commit-lock structural test could no longer parse the intentional `_gate-mutation-guard` prerequisite. The push guard also printed abbreviated 12-character family IDs even though the repair command accepts only complete 64-character identities.
- **Root cause**: Passive FreeLLMAPI replay contracts and persistent-vector trie primitives remained embedded in already dense implementation modules. Separately, the structural parser assumed a prerequisite-free target declaration, and the status surface shortened an identifier that was also an operator input.
- **Fix applied**: Passive three-arm value contracts and persistent-vector trie primitives now have cohesive modules while their established imports retain object identity. The lock test accepts prerequisites and independently requires the active-gate mutation guard. CI status and push blockers now emit complete copyable family identities.
- **Evidence**: The maintainability count and lock regression pass without raising either threshold; the behavior replay passes 72/72; persistent-vector coverage is 98% for both measured files; the complete FreeLLMAPI three-arm profile passes 77/77 at 92% aggregate with every file above 75%; scoped lint, types, docstrings, and TDD checks are green. Exact commit, full gate, and hosted proof remain required.
- **Practitioner evidence**: `docs/features/CI_FAILURE_LEDGER.md` retains the long-lived GitHub Actions reports about duplicate candidates, exact-head identity, partial reruns, and artifact loss. Those reports reinforce using immutable, directly reusable identifiers at every repair boundary rather than asking an operator to reconstruct hidden state.
- **Lesson**: Validate the merged candidate rather than only its parents, and never abbreviate an identity on the surface that an adjacent command requires as exact input.

### 2026-10-05 — (replayed locally; replacement hosted proof required) Active-workflow snapshot understated the terminal failure set

- **What happened**: While Build/Release run `37381218767` was still active, three completed unit jobs exposed four assertions and were reported as the complete repair set. The terminal run actually had four failed jobs: `other` batch 22 later added both `fake-local` and `fake-azure` cases of `test_self_improvement_uses_common_todo_ranking_compute_and_real_edit`, each observing `['present']` instead of the required `['present', 'absent']` sandbox-state sequence.
- **Root cause**: Per-job diagnostics were correctly available before workflow completion, but an interim snapshot was incorrectly described as terminal completeness while a sibling test job was still running. The diagnostic boundary and the release-verdict boundary were conflated.
- **Fix applied**: Terminal reconciliation now enumerates every job before making a completeness claim, then extracts every failed terminal job independently. The two late self-improvement cases were replayed together on candidate `5142ba735f66fbf074a7ec02d80d0d2ced0b582c`; both pass without rerunning or rewriting the failed hosted attempt.
- **Evidence**: The immutable run summary reports 4 failed, 20 passed, and 4 skipped jobs. Job `112007987360` retained exactly the two parametrized failures above; their exact candidate replay passes 2/2. The terminal failure inventory is therefore six assertions: four structural/unit assertions plus two cross-provider self-improvement assertions. Replacement hosted proof remains required before either repaired family is called resolved.
- **Practitioner evidence**: GitHub CLI [issue #4575](https://github.com/cli/cli/issues/4575) demonstrates that one completed job's logs can remain coupled to the broader workflow lifecycle, while GitHub's [workflow jobs REST API](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/actions/workflow-jobs) exposes job-scoped evidence. The smaller boundary is appropriate for early diagnosis, but only the terminal workflow inventory can prove completeness.
- **Lesson**: An active-run inventory is provisional even when every currently failed job has been fully diagnosed. “Complete” requires terminal enumeration of all sibling jobs, not just complete extraction from the siblings that have already failed.

### 2026-10-05 — (resolved locally; replacement exact gate required) Candidate accumulated four structural-contract failures

- **What happened**: The three unit jobs completed at diagnosis time in hosted run `37381218767` retained four exact structural regressions: `event_loop/loop.py` grew to 5,594 lines against the 5,500-line ceiling; that same drift broke the v0.1.1 repair contract; two additional functions crossed the repository's long-function budget; and a legacy binary test still required `ubuntu-latest` after the hosted-capacity repair deliberately pinned Ubuntu 24.04.
- **Root cause**: General work-type routing, prompt resolution, playbook selection, and cost estimation remained embedded in the event-loop orchestration monolith. Unrelated feature additions therefore consumed its file and function budgets. Separately, two tests owned the Linux runner-image invariant and only the newer capacity contract had been updated.
- **Fix applied**: The provider- and domain-neutral routing helpers now live in `event_loop/task_routing.py`; `loop.py` retains compatibility exports so callers and patch points do not break. Tick checkpoint restoration and extended daemon-state initialization are cohesive helpers, reducing two functions below the enforced threshold without raising any budget. The legacy cross-platform test now asserts the same explicit Ubuntu 24.04 image as the canonical hosted-capacity contract.
- **Evidence**: All four exact hosted failures were reproduced locally before repair and now pass together. The routing compatibility and behavioral slice passes 64/64; the broader routing coverage replay passes 135 with one intentional skip at 97% branch-aware coverage; scoped Ruff and strict mypy are green. The full affected 310-test replay, task/resource checks, clean commit, integration, and replacement exact gate remain required.
- **Practitioner evidence**: `docs/features/HOSTED_RUNNER_CAPACITY.md` records long-lived runner-acquisition reports and GitHub's runner-image lifecycle that require an explicit image pin. `docs/features/UNIVERSAL_TASK_RUNTIME.md` records FreeLLMAPI operator reports #880 and #1218 that motivate keeping provider discovery separate from stable universal routing rather than growing domain-specific branches inside the event loop.
- **Lesson**: A shared orchestrator should compose small universal policies, not own every policy body. Structural tests that duplicate an invariant must point at the same exact value or they become delayed failures instead of independent protection.

### 2026-10-05 — (resolved locally; replacement hosted proof required) Completed job logs were hidden behind an active workflow

- **What happened**: Build run `37381218767` had completed failed test jobs while a sibling job was still running. The existing diagnostic target delegated to `gh run view --log --job`, which refused to return any job log until the entire workflow completed. The first workaround also bounded extraction to one match, hiding a second independent failure in the same shard.
- **Root cause**: The GitHub CLI command models logs at workflow completion even though GitHub's REST API exposes the log for each completed job. Gludd coupled diagnostic availability to the broadest lifecycle boundary and hard-coded the extractor's match count rather than making the safety bound explicit.
- **Fix applied**: `ci-job-failure-context` still proves the numeric job belongs to the requested immutable run, then downloads that completed job through the authenticated job-log REST endpoint into the external resource namespace. Empty responses and missing patterns fail closed. `MAX_MATCHES` is now a validated, contract-registered bound, defaulting to five, so all independently failing batches can be surfaced without unbounded output.
- **Evidence**: The failing-first contract required the job endpoint, current API version, empty-log rejection, and the explicit match bound. Against still-active run `37381218767`, the repaired target retrieved two `SHARD-FAIL` records from `unit-1b` and one each from `unit-2` and `unit-3b`, exposing the four then-terminal test failures before workflow completion. The terminal reconciliation above separately owns the later `other`-job failures. Focused tests, target-contract validation, and replacement hosted evidence remain required.
- **Practitioner evidence**: GitHub CLI [issue #4575](https://github.com/cli/cli/issues/4575) reports that `gh run view --log --job` waits for the whole workflow despite the selected job being complete. Related operator reports [#4712](https://github.com/cli/cli/issues/4712), [#11059](https://github.com/cli/cli/issues/11059), and [#11109](https://github.com/cli/cli/issues/11109) document job-log availability and retrieval inconsistencies. GitHub's [workflow jobs REST API](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/actions/workflow-jobs) is the authoritative per-job lifecycle boundary.
- **Lesson**: Diagnosis must bind to the smallest immutable owner that has actually completed. Safety bounds belong in validated inputs, not hidden constants that silently discard sibling failures.

### 2026-10-05 — (resolved locally; replacement exact gate required) Duplicate background launch clobbered the observed gate PID

- **What happened**: A diagnostic `make -n gate-background GATE_TIMEOUT=7200` unexpectedly executed the recursive recipe. The existing-gate check printed that PID `81162` was live, but its `exit 0` ended only that recipe shell; Make then ran the next recipe line, started a duplicate, and overwrote `.gate-background.pid`. The gate lock rejected the duplicate, but `gate-background-observed` reread the shared PID file, concluded the original was finished, returned, and the managed runner reaped the still-progressing diagnostic gate during its first unit batch. That gate was already invalid as candidate evidence because HEAD had changed beneath it, but the ownership loss was independent and reproducible.
- **Root cause**: Three boundaries were incorrectly coupled: duplicate admission and launch were separate recipe shells; direct `$(MAKE)` references cause GNU Make to execute recursive recipe lines even under `-n`; and the observed waiter followed a mutable global PID file instead of the exact PID published by its launch.
- **Fix applied**: Duplicate inspection, stale-owner handling, launch, PID publication, and timeout ownership now share one shell boundary. The expanded Make command is captured before recipe execution, so both launcher recipes remain ordinary dry-run commands without losing the intended Make binary. The observed wrapper captures the published PID once and passes it through `GATE_EXPECTED_PID`; status polling uses that immutable owner even if the shared file later changes.
- **Evidence**: Three new regressions failed first against the split shell, direct recursive references, and missing exact-PID handoff. The focused target suite passes 16/16, including real subprocess-backed duplicate refusal with an unchanged PID record and a real `make -n` proof that creates neither a PID file nor a log directory. The broader ownership, cleanup, exact-SHA status, observability, and phase-marker replay passes 111/111. Scoped Ruff, Markdown, Make validation, the 181-target contract, task ledger/integrity/registration, resource ownership, and the real validation-mode behavioral example are green. Commit evidence and a replacement exact-candidate gate remain required.
- **Practitioner evidence**: GNU Make documents that recipe lines containing the special `MAKE` variable execute even under `-n` in [How the `MAKE` Variable Works](https://www.gnu.org/software/make/manual/html_node/MAKE-Variable.html). GitHub Actions runner [issue #4601](https://github.com/actions/runner/issues/4601) demonstrates that managed runners explicitly clean descendant processes, so an observed parent cannot relinquish ownership before its exact child publishes a terminal receipt.
- **Lesson**: A refusal is a control-flow boundary, not a log message. Admission and launch must be atomic, dry-run behavior must be tested against the real target, and an observer must retain the immutable identity it launched rather than following a replaceable pointer.

### 2026-10-05 — (resolved locally; replacement exact gate required) Development history changed under an active candidate gate

- **What happened**: The observed full gate started from clean `development` commit `c35a78038` and progressed into integration preflights. While its live owner lock was present, an unrelated future-release worktree merged documentation and moved the same checkout's HEAD to `8c4843b71`. The working tree remained clean, so ordinary dirty-tree checks did not detect the candidate-identity change. The running gate is retained as diagnostic evidence but cannot prove either exact commit.
- **Root cause**: The gate lock prevented a second gate and protected the gate from watchdog termination, but Git mutation targets did not consult it. Commit-shaped targets serialized only with other commits, and the merge wrapper enforced strategy but not active candidate ownership. A clean worktree therefore said nothing about immutable HEAD identity during a long run.
- **Fix applied**: `gate_run_lock.py assert-inactive` now distinguishes missing, malformed, stale, and live ownership. It fails closed on an unreadable record or live owner and atomically reclaims only a dead owner. The common commit lock, merge-strategy guard, agent/gated merge targets, checkout, and cherry-pick all depend on one worktree-local `_gate-mutation-guard`. A live main-checkout gate therefore freezes its own Git history, while isolated feature worktrees—with their own lock paths—remain free to prepare fixes.
- **Evidence**: Three failing-first regressions showed the unsupported lock action and unguarded headers. The expanded lock suite passes 11/11. Branch-aware production coverage is 94%, with the measured file above 75%; Ruff, separate strict source/test mypy, Markdown, Make validation, the 181-target contract, task ledger/integrity/registration, and resource ownership are green. Against the real active main gate, the feature worktree guard refused mutation and named owner PID `81162`; the same guard passed against its own absent lock. Commit evidence and a replacement exact gate remain required.
- **Practitioner evidence**: Git's [worktree porcelain contract](https://git-scm.com/docs/git-worktree#_porcelain_format) defines each registered checkout as a distinct path and supplies the boundary used here. GitHub Actions runner [issue #4601](https://github.com/actions/runner/issues/4601) documents why runner-owned process cleanup must follow explicit ownership rather than snapshots, while psutil [issue #2335](https://github.com/giampaolo/psutil/issues/2335) records the wrong-owner risk of command-line inference.
- **Lesson**: Clean is not immutable. A long candidate run must own both its process tree and the checkout's history-mutation boundary until terminal evidence is published.

### 2026-10-05 — (resolved locally; live observed gate required) Managed command completion reaped detached gate owners

- **What happened**: Two clean `make gate-background GATE_TIMEOUT=7200` launches returned a PID, then that PID disappeared before `_gate-run-lock-acquire` emitted a byte. Each newest gate log existed but was empty, `.gate-status` retained the prior diagnostic verdict, the PID file became stale, and the project census showed no gate descendant. The second attempt ran in an otherwise idle project namespace, excluding test contention as the repeatable condition.
- **Root cause**: `gate-background` intentionally returns as soon as `nohup` starts its recursive Make child. In a managed command runner, completion of the root command is also the lifecycle boundary for its descendants; `nohup` changes terminal-signal handling but does not transfer ownership outside that runner. The exact external reaper is not exposed, so Gludd treats the observed caller-lifetime coupling—not an inferred process identity—as the actionable cause.
- **Fix applied**: The existing nonblocking target remains unchanged for interactive use. New `make gate-background-observed` starts it and then invokes the existing heartbeat-producing `gate-wait` from the same root Make process, keeping the automation owner alive until the gate publishes a terminal result. It forwards explicit timeout/poll variables, preserves the child's result, and has a side-effect-free validation mode tracked by the Make target contract.
- **Evidence**: The failing-first regression reported the target absent. The complete background-target replay passes 11/11 and the broader cleanup/timeout/observability replay passes 82/82; scoped Ruff and strict mypy are green. The target contract passes with 181 entries, and `make gate-background-observed GATE_TIMEOUT=7200 GATE_POLL_INTERVAL=60 GATE_BACKGROUND_OBSERVED_VALIDATE_ONLY=1` prints the bounded plan without launching work. The real observed gate remains required.
- **Practitioner evidence**: GNU Make's [POSIX jobserver contract](https://www.gnu.org/software/make/manual/html_node/POSIX-Jobserver.html) documents that recursive children depend on resources owned by the calling Make process. GitHub Actions runner [issue #4601](https://github.com/actions/runner/issues/4601) shows that automation runners explicitly census and clean orphan descendants, while runner [issue #1309](https://github.com/actions/runner/issues/1309) documents externally selected termination signals. Together they support retaining an observable owner instead of assuming `nohup` transfers lifecycle authority.
- **Lesson**: Detachment is not ownership. Automation must keep one visible root process alive through the terminal receipt whenever the execution environment owns descendant cleanup.

### 2026-10-05 — (resolved locally; detached exact-candidate gate required) Legacy watchdogs could still signal new gate descendants

- **What happened**: After repository-wide gate-tree exclusion and watchdog PID ownership were repaired, a complete diagnostic gate again received external `SIGTERM`: the integration wrapper exited 143 after roughly 488 seconds and the serial unit runner received signal 15 immediately after starting batch 13. Every completed unit batch was green, and the exact interrupted batch had no assertion failure before the signal. The evidence still does not identify the sender, so the incident is not attributed to a specific live process.
- **Root cause**: The repository-inventory repair protects gates only in watchdog processes running the new code. A task watchdog already running from an older linked worktree still uses its historical command-line exclusion, which ignores any candidate containing the case-sensitive token `watchdog`, and can observe new gate descendants whose commands contain only `pytest`, `make gate`, or a shard-runner name. Updating one checkout cannot atomically upgrade every already-running linked-worktree controller.
- **Fix applied**: Every long-lived layer of a detached release gate now carries the backward-compatible lowercase `watchdog-owned-gate` marker: the root recursive Make command, integration wrapper and its pytest child, serial shard runner, and each isolated or batched pytest child. Current watchdogs still use repository/process-tree ownership; the marker is a compatibility shield for historical controllers, not the primary identity mechanism. Ordinary non-gate invocations retain their previous command shape.
- **Evidence**: Three failing-first regressions proved the marker was absent from the gate entrypoints, integration child, and serial children. The repaired focused cases pass 3/3, and the expanded watchdog, integration-wrapper, serial-runner, gate-concurrency, and observability replay passes 287/287. Branch-aware coverage passes at 89% aggregate, with the integration wrapper at 92%, the serial runner at 89%, and no measured file below 75%; scoped Ruff, separate strict source/test mypy, Markdown, Make validation, the 180-target contract, task integrity/registration, and resource ownership are green. A detached complete exact-candidate gate remains required because the foreground diagnostic run was externally interrupted.
- **Practitioner evidence**: pytest-timeout [issue #159](https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest-timeout/issues/159) documents child processes surviving controller teardown; psutil [issue #2335](https://github.com/giampaolo/psutil/issues/2335) warns that command-line inference can select the wrong process; psutil [issue #2534](https://github.com/giampaolo/psutil/issues/2534) describes process-group ownership surviving intermediate exits; and Git's [worktree porcelain contract](https://git-scm.com/docs/git-worktree#_porcelain_format) remains the maintained repository-membership source.
- **Lesson**: A rolling control-plane repair needs a compatibility boundary that older controllers already understand. The boundary must cover every descendant capable of outliving its parent, while exact repository and process ownership remains authoritative for new code.

### 2026-10-05 — (resolved locally; restart and exact-candidate gate required) Plugin cleanup erased another watchdog's ownership record

- **What happened**: While tracing an unexplained external `SIGTERM` during the v0.1.1 gate, `make ps-gludd` could not show either watchdog daemon and the task-watchdog PID record was absent. The evidence does not identify the sender of that signal, but it exposed a separate deterministic ownership defect: the OpenCode session watchdog deleted the independent Python task watchdog's PID file whenever any OpenCode session ended.
- **Root cause**: `.opencode/plugins/watchdog.ts` treated `/tmp/gludd-task-watchdog.pid` as plugin-owned cleanup state even though `scripts/task_watchdog.py` owns that process and record. At the same time, `make ps-gludd` omitted both `task_watchdog.py` and `agent_watchdog.py`, making the resulting live-without-record or record-without-visibility state difficult to diagnose.
- **Fix applied**: Session cleanup now removes only `/tmp/gludd-watchdog.pid`, the file its own plugin creates. Runtime regressions prove an externally owned task-watchdog record survives `session.deleted`; structural coverage rejects future ownership claims; and `make ps-gludd` includes both watchdog daemons in its namespaced census.
- **Evidence**: The failing-first focused replay reported 2 failures and 43 passes. The repaired focused replay passes 70/70; hook runtime passes 151 tests with 18 intentional skips; OpenCode E2E passes 44/44; all 36 plugins load; the manifest passes 108/108; Node 26 compatibility passes 5/5; and plugin syntax, Ruff, markdown, Make validation, the 180-target contract, task integrity, ledger validation, registration, and the real `make ps-gludd` example are green. Commit and exact-candidate evidence remain required before release promotion.
- **Practitioner evidence**: pytest-timeout [issue #159](https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest-timeout/issues/159) documents orphaned subprocesses when lifecycle ownership is split. psutil [issue #2335](https://github.com/giampaolo/psutil/issues/2335) warns that command-line inference can select the wrong process, while [issue #2534](https://github.com/giampaolo/psutil/issues/2534) describes process-group ownership surviving intermediate exits. Git's [worktree porcelain contract](https://git-scm.com/docs/git-worktree#_porcelain_format) remains Gludd's repository-membership boundary.
- **Lesson**: A cleanup hook may delete only the process record it creates and owns. Diagnostic process inventories must expose every destructive controller whose decisions can affect the gate. This plugin change requires an OpenCode restart before the running session adopts it.

### 2026-10-05 — (resolved locally; exact-candidate gate required) Linked-worktree watchdogs could terminate another checkout's gate

- **What happened**: A complete local v0.1.1 gate received external `SIGTERM` twice while its integration and unit shards were making progress. The interrupted unit batch passed 411/411 when replayed alone. No current kill receipt survived, so that specific signal cannot be attributed conclusively, but inspection exposed a deterministic cross-worktree ownership defect in the only watchdog designed to send the same signal to old test processes.
- **Root cause**: `scripts/task_watchdog.py` scans the host-global process table when the host-global deadline ledger contains a stale entry, but protected only `.gate-background.pid` and `.gate-logs/gate-run.lock` beneath its own `GLUDD_WORKSPACE_ROOT`. A watchdog launched from any linked checkout could therefore classify a healthy gate owned by another registered checkout as an unrelated old `make gate` or `pytest` process.
- **Fix applied**: Before any destructive scan, the watchdog consumes the existing Git-porcelain repository inventory used by active-work status and passes every registered root to process classification. It excludes the union of every live foreground/background gate tree. If repository discovery fails, it sends no signal; malformed individual gate records still grant no exemption, and unrelated stale processes remain eligible.
- **Evidence**: The failing-first regression raised `TypeError` because the scanner had no repository ownership input. The repaired suite passes 42/42, including linked-worktree exclusion, production poll propagation, direct-script imports, malformed records, unrelated stale work, and fail-safe inventory failure. Exact-candidate gate replay remains required.
- **Practitioner evidence**: pytest-timeout [issue #159](https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest-timeout/issues/159) documents orphaned subprocesses and recommends an owning wrapper. psutil [issue #2335](https://github.com/giampaolo/psutil/issues/2335) warns that command-line heuristics can kill the wrong process, and [issue #2534](https://github.com/giampaolo/psutil/issues/2534) explains why process-group ownership survives intermediate exits. Git's [worktree porcelain contract](https://git-scm.com/docs/git-worktree#_porcelain_format) provides the maintained repository membership boundary.
- **Lesson**: A destructive controller's ownership domain must be at least as broad as its observation and trigger domains. Host-global deadlines plus a host-global process scan cannot be paired with checkout-local exemptions.

### 2026-10-04 — (resolved locally; exact-candidate gate required) Stale `gate-background` timeout killers caused false `GATE_TIMEOUT`

- **What happened**: A fresh `make gate-background GATE_TIMEOUT=7200` was terminated after roughly five minutes with `.gate-status` showing `GATE_TIMEOUT`, even though the new timeout killer was configured to sleep for 7200s and the default is 3600s.
- **Root cause**: Each `make gate-background` invocation spawns a detached timeout-killer shell that sleeps for `GATE_TIMEOUT` seconds and then kills the PID currently recorded in `.gate-background.pid`. Because the PID file is overwritten by every new launch, an old killer from a prior session woke up, found the current gate's PID, sent `SIGTERM`, and wrote `GATE_TIMEOUT` to `.gate-status`.
- **Fix applied**: Makefile `gate-background` and `gate-lite-background` now capture the expected PID before the killer sleeps, and the killer only terminates the gate if the PID file still contains that same expected PID. This prevents a stale killer from a prior launch from killing a newer gate. Committed on `development` as `f99a081e9a6a2c01981f095582f93e21070d0021`.
- **Evidence**: After killing stale killers (PIDs 33999, 34000, 65208, 65209, 97243, 97244) and launching a fresh gate, `make gate-wait` ran for roughly 2h50m and the gate completed with `=== GATE: PASSED ===` and attestation state `cabb5b1601cd6d7ab8dc20c56ff223dc8f08a08c35c7367e3f4f9a05e2f75e16`. The PID-guard fix passed `make collect-check` and was pushed to `sandboxcom/development`.
- **Lesson**: `make gate-kill` terminates the active gate tree but does not reap detached timeout-killer processes from earlier launches. A background gate launch must either use a per-run killer identity or proactively kill prior killers before starting a new one.

### 2026-09-30 — (resolved locally; exact-candidate gate required) Warning review checker treated a feature-parent graph as merge-new

- **What happened**: The exact feature-branch gate passed, but the post-merge `development` gate retained one otherwise isolated preflight failure: `check-pyinstaller-warning-reviews` demanded a new x86_64 receipt for digest `d4fcb35b…`. That graph was already accepted on the feature parent and therefore was not introduced by the merge.
- **Root cause**: The checker used only `HEAD^`, Git's first parent, when the working policy matched `HEAD`. On a two-parent merge this erased accepted identities carried by the second parent and converted ordinary inherited state into a false “new digest” finding.
- **Fix applied**: The checker now enumerates every immutable parent SHA, validates each parent policy, and unions their accepted primary and alternate graph identities before calculating additions. Single-parent, working-tree, root-commit, malformed-policy, and complete-receipt behavior is unchanged; a digest absent from every parent still fails closed.
- **Evidence**: The merge-parent regression failed first by omitting the first parent's accepted digest from history. The repaired focused suite passes 25/25, and the real `check-pyinstaller-warning-reviews` preflight now passes on merge commit `3d142a90937f68722827ed93f044ba2e57d0acb3`. A replacement exact-candidate gate remains required because source changed after the prior gate.
- **Practitioner evidence**: GitHub Community [discussion #56153](https://github.com/orgs/community/discussions/56153) identifies multiple parents as the defining merge property. The CodeQL paper [Tracking Static Analysis Violations over Time](https://codeql.github.com/publications/tracking-analysis-violations.pdf) states the corresponding analysis invariant: a merge introduces a violation only when every parent lacks it.
- **Lesson**: Any “new at this commit” admission check must quantify over all merge parents. First-parent history is useful for mainline presentation, but it is not sufficient provenance for inherited policy state.

### 2026-09-29 — (resolved locally; exact-candidate gate required) Delegate E2E inherited the live durable dispatch ledger

- **What happened**: The complete non-short-circuit candidate gate passed every preflight, static phase, integration test, later unit batch, coverage aggregation, and smoke check, but retained one failure: `tests/e2e/test_delegate_e2e.py::test_dispatch_resets_streak`. Its synthetic `task` dispatch was denied as a duplicate of an earlier `task\ndo work` owner in the repository's durable `.gludd/dispatch-ledger.json`.
- **Root cause**: The E2E helper isolated its streak and disengage files but did not redirect `GLUDD_DISPATCH_DEDUP_STATE`. Adding durable content-addressed ownership therefore made an unrelated streak-reset test consume real cross-process project state. The assertion was still valid; its environment no longer represented an isolated test session.
- **Fix applied**: The streak-reset test now points the dispatch ledger at its function-scoped `tmp_path`. All three synthetic dispatch tools share that isolated ledger, while production defaults and the dedicated persistent-dedup suite retain fail-closed cross-process ownership.
- **Evidence**: The full gate failure is the failing-first proof and its machine ledger contains exactly one `SHARD-FAIL`. The exact failed node now passes 1/1; the complete delegate E2E plus persistent-dedup runtime replay passes 21/21, including exact-prompt and task-ID duplicate denial, failed-dispatch retry, completed-dispatch denial, live-lock refusal, and dead-lock reclamation. A replacement exact-candidate gate remains required.
- **Practitioner evidence**: Pytest issue [#11790](https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/issues/11790) documents that apparently unique temporary paths can still collide across concurrent invocations unless the run boundary is explicitly unique. The pytest-xdist discussion [#1213](https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest-xdist/discussions/1213) recommends temporary files plus locking when shared state is intentional. Gludd now applies both halves: function-scoped state for unrelated behavior tests, durable locked state only for ownership tests.
- **Lesson**: A temporary fixture does not isolate state that still points outside it. Every stateful plugin test must redirect every durable state channel it can reach, while dedicated ownership tests must separately prove persistence and locking.

### 2026-09-29 — (resolved locally; exact-candidate gate required) Disk cleanup counted but categorically protected the invoking caches

- **What happened**: The replacement complete gate retained `disk-cleanup-preflight` as a failure even after its safe cleanup pass removed 735.5 MiB from the shared uv cache. The canonical recheck still reported 133.0 MiB of generated scratch and 92% volume use. Classification showed 104.125 MiB came from `.pytest_cache`, `.mypy_cache`, and `.ruff_cache` in the exact invoking worktree, while the invoking and exact registered main checkouts retained 2.6 GiB and 2.5 GiB regenerable Terraform provider caches. The first live provider-cache proof also exposed that removing the whole directory deleted its tracked `.gitkeep` marker. After those defects were repaired and the complete gate passed, the guarded commit reproduced one final convergence gap: the process scan classified its own synchronous `make`/pre-commit controller ancestor as an unrelated active worktree user, so it refused the caches the hook itself had just generated.
- **Root cause**: `scripts/check_disk_usage.py` intentionally counts disposable caches in every registered worktree and checks the shared repository volume, but `scripts/automatic_disk_cleanup.py` passed the invoking path in `protected_paths`, categorically excluded the main checkout, and had no safe tier for either checkout's declared provider cache. The preflight therefore measured bytes it refused to reclaim and could not converge without model-directed cleanup; whole-directory removal also confused generated cache contents with the tracked directory marker. The first invoking-tier implementation then treated every path-matching PID alike, without separating the cleanup controller's verified parent chain from independent work.
- **Fix applied**: A dedicated registered-worktree tier selects only the three invoking direct-child test/type caches and the exact `infra/terraform/.plugin-cache`; a linked-worktree invocation may additionally clear only that exact cache in the canonical main checkout. It requires an exact unlocked, attached Git registration and exact approved identity, validates every path component, scans processes, refreshes registration, repeats process inspection immediately before every removal, and refuses symlinks, non-directories, races, arbitrary cache selections, or ambiguous inspection. For this invoking tier only, each scan subtracts the cleanup process's freshly inspected ancestor PID chain; any sibling, child, unrelated process, failed process inspection, or newly appearing owner still blocks removal. Provider entries are cleared while `.gitkeep` is retained. `.venv`, Terraform state and locks, source, application state, gate evidence, and release artifacts remain ineligible. Repetitive skip/refusal output is capped at 20 details per kind and pass with an omitted count.
- **Evidence**: The invoking-cache, exact-main-cache, marker-preservation, bounded-output, controller-ancestor, and unrelated-process tests each failed first. The complete cleanup test file passes 97/97; the new regression proves one allowlisted controller ancestor permits reclamation while one additional PID blocks it. Earlier focused coverage measured the production file at 86% branch-aware coverage with no file below 75%, and scoped Ruff plus strict mypy are green. A live `make check-disk CHECK_DISK_VALIDATE_ONLY=0` replay under its active Make controller removed all three invoking caches, both exact provider caches, and 498 MiB of idle uv cache, then reduced the canonical readings from 132.3 MiB/91% to a healthy 29.2 MiB/89%. A replacement exact complete gate and guarded-commit replay remain required because the source changed after the prior gate passed.
- **Practitioner evidence**: [pytest discussion 10325](https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/discussions/10325) records long-lived demand for automatic cleanup of retained test directories, while [uv issue 11694](https://github.com/astral-sh/uv/issues/11694) documents cleanup racing a live process. The implementation combines the useful automation from the former with repeated ownership checks required by the latter.
- **Lesson**: A safety controller must own a convergent action for every byte its pressure sensor counts. Protection and cleanup policy may differ by resource class, but a measured class cannot be both release-blocking and permanently unreclaimable.

### 2026-09-29 — (resolved locally; exact-candidate gate required) Gate contract tests confused diagnostics with release assets

- **What happened**: The complete exact-SHA gate at `197769f4b4225feacdef229b68d34eb11a4c01b5` ran every phase and retained 17 reproducible test failures. Six assertions still expected full-gate checks to be direct `gate:` prerequisites after the non-short-circuit ledger refactor; five treated the first workflow artifact as a publishable release asset even when it was failure-diagnostic evidence; the rest exposed stale Molecule path ordering, an omitted dispatch-ledger configuration row, an incomplete Node 24 action set, fixed-width Makefile parsing, a newly documented pipeline variable, and a `.PHONY` parser that consumed the next target after a single-line declaration.
- **Root cause**: Structural tests encoded incidental source layout instead of the canonical contracts. Artifact tests classified by action type rather than admission name, gate tests classified by one header rather than `GATE_PREFLIGHT_TARGETS` plus its runner, and two Makefile tests used unbounded or fixed-width text scanning.
- **Fix applied**: Full-gate assertions now verify the canonical preflight list and non-short-circuit runner. Workflow tests classify only `gludd-*` names as release assets, require those uploads to follow successful smoke checks, and separately require non-release diagnostics to survive failures with `always()`. The remaining assertions now follow the ephemeral-first Molecule path, complete environment/action/target contracts, recipe boundaries, and actual multiline `.PHONY` continuation state.
- **Evidence**: The identical 17-file reproduction set now passes 378/378, including the previously cached but non-reproducible webhook concurrency node. The exact clean committed full gate remains required before this repair becomes release evidence.
- **Practitioner evidence**: `actions/upload-artifact` issue [#585](https://github.com/actions/upload-artifact/issues/585) and [#328](https://github.com/actions/upload-artifact/issues/328) document long-lived reliance on unconditional uploads for failure logs. GitHub Community discussion [#206753](https://github.com/orgs/community/discussions/206753) documents that workflow artifacts become release assets only through explicit release-job download and publication. Runner issue [#4295](https://github.com/actions/runner/issues/4295) documents persistent runtime-version ambiguity avoided by native Node 24 action pins.
- **Lesson**: Test the admission boundary, not the shared transport. Diagnostics and release assets may use the same upload action but have opposite failure semantics; likewise, a gate check belongs to the full gate because the canonical runner owns it, not because its name happens to appear on one target header.

### 2026-09-29 — (resolved locally; exact-candidate gate required) Resource-owning repairs omitted their exact inventory entries

- **What happened**: The complete preflight wave found three new temporary-artifact acquisitions in the Ansible runtime refresh, CI failure ledger, and PyInstaller graph receipt writers. Each implementation already used atomic replacement and failure-path cleanup, but their exact hashes were absent from the tracked resource-ownership inventory.
- **Root cause**: The three features and their focused tests were committed without regenerating the same-commit ownership inventory. Their cleanup was correct, but the repository-wide fail-closed baseline correctly treated unreviewed acquisitions as release blockers.
- **Fix applied**: The acquisition/teardown pairs were reviewed first, then the checker alone regenerated the counted inventory. The inventory now binds all three owners, source hashes, atomic acquisitions, and teardown evidence; read-only validation remains the default and passes with 212 resources.
- **Evidence**: `make check-resource-ownership` passes with 28 async tasks, 72 clients, 31 processes, nine services, and 72 temporary artifacts. The checker regression suite passes 32/32. The interrupted exact-candidate gate reached 56% of integration after retaining this as its only observed preflight failure; because the process was interrupted, that partial run is not a gate verdict and a complete replay remains required.
- **Practitioner evidence**: The detect-secrets [baseline identity design](https://github.com/Yelp/detect-secrets/blob/master/docs/design.md#potentialsecret) and issue [#212](https://github.com/Yelp/detect-secrets/issues/212) show why semantic evidence and generated baselines must move together without using line coordinates as identity. CPython issue [#79325](https://github.com/python/cpython/issues/79325) documents long-lived temporary-directory cleanup failures that justify retaining exact owner-side teardown evidence.
- **Lesson**: A resource-owning code change and its exact ownership inventory are one review unit. Focused behavior tests cannot substitute for the repository-wide acquisition-to-teardown admission gate.

### 2026-09-29 — (resolved locally; exact-candidate gate required) Gate prerequisites hid every failure after the first one

- **What happened**: The replacement release gate encountered generated third-party collection files during task registration and stopped immediately. None of the remaining preflights, lint, type checking, full test suite, or smoke phase ran, so the hour-long attempt returned one defect instead of the complete repair set it was supposed to collect.
- **Root cause**: Thirty-three independent checks were declared as ordinary prerequisites of `gate`. GNU Make stops the target when a prerequisite fails; `--keep-going` can visit independent siblings, but it still does not execute the failed target's recipe. The implementation therefore contradicted the documented non-short-circuit build policy.
- **Fix applied**: `gate` now invokes an explicit preflight runner that executes every named check, streams each phase, writes a durable PASS/FAIL row for every target, marks the final gate failed when any row fails, and still proceeds through lint, claims, dead-code, environment, hook, end-to-end, enforcement, coverage, type, collection, test, and smoke phases. A synthetic pass/fail/pass behavioral fixture proves that a middle failure cannot suppress the later check or be reported as success.
- **Evidence**: The failing-first structural and executable regressions cover target topology, retained status, failure propagation, and later-target execution. The combined gate-aggregation and Molecule-path replay passes 57/57. An exact committed-source full gate remains required before release evidence is terminal.
- **Practitioner evidence**: The long-lived Stack Overflow discussions [“Makefile: --ignore-errors vs --keep-going”](https://stackoverflow.com/questions/53039550/makefile-ignore-errors-vs-keep-going), [“Make ignore errors: difference between -i and -k”](https://stackoverflow.com/questions/53890710/make-ignore-errors-what-is-the-difference-between-i-and-k), and [“Define continue if error policy directly in target dependencies”](https://stackoverflow.com/questions/53760185/define-continue-if-error-policy-directly-in-target-dependencies) document why `-i` loses failure truth and `-k` alone cannot run the parent recipe after a failed prerequisite.
- **Lesson**: A complete build verdict needs an explicit result ledger. Independent checks must all run, while any failure remains sticky through the final exit status; global error-ignoring flags are not evidence aggregation.

### 2026-09-29 — (resolved locally; committed replay required) Molecule installed third-party dependencies into project source

- **What happened**: The real exact-SHA `binary_smoke_linux` scenario passed end to end, but Galaxy installed 929 Ansible and Community files beneath the repository's owned `collections/` tree. The next gate correctly rejected those unregistered paths, and the generated namespaces had to be removed before trustworthy validation could continue.
- **Root cause**: `ANSIBLE_COLLECTIONS_PATH` listed the writable project collection root before Molecule's run-scoped ephemeral dependency directory. Ansible Galaxy installs into the first configured collection path, so a read/resolve ordering choice accidentally became persistent source mutation.
- **Fix applied**: Molecule now puts its ephemeral dependency directory first and the project-owned collection root second. Third-party requirements remain isolated to the scenario lifecycle, while Gludd's own collection remains resolvable. Existing cleanup traps remove the ephemeral directory on success and failure, and a regression prohibits the unsafe path order.
- **Evidence**: Exact commit `3e5a4b93116d4ff6a3a26bc488acadfe01f54f9c` built the Python 3.12.14/UV 0.12.19 aarch64 ELF, passed the exact 1,329-edge warning-graph audit, and passed the full packaged-daemon Molecule scenario before exposing the ownership defect. The combined repaired path and complete-preflight replay passes 57/57; an exact committed replay must now prove the source tree remains clean.
- **Practitioner evidence**: Ansible's [`galaxy.py`](https://github.com/ansible/ansible/blob/devel/lib/ansible/cli/galaxy.py) explicitly chooses the first collection path for installation. Ansible issues [#68621](https://github.com/ansible/ansible/issues/68621) and [#72628](https://github.com/ansible/ansible/issues/72628) record persistent confusion and unusable installs caused by collection-path selection. Molecule issue [#3999](https://github.com/ansible/molecule/issues/3999) recommends an explicit `ANSIBLE_COLLECTIONS_PATH` when its generated configuration is insufficient.
- **Lesson**: Search paths are also write-ownership policy. Put disposable dependency roots before project source, prove cleanup, and reject any test tool that mutates tracked-source namespaces as a side effect.

### 2026-09-29 — (resolved locally; exact-candidate gate required) Dirty-tree artifact replay silently built the old commit

- **What happened**: After the aarch64 warning policy and its exact-delta receipt were repaired in the working tree, `make build-linux-executable` spent another full container build and reported the old `b1f5847a…` policy failure. The target had archived committed `HEAD`, not the uncommitted repair, without identifying that source boundary in its output.
- **Root cause**: Building from an immutable Git tree is the correct release boundary, but the target accepted a dirty worktree and did not print the resolved source SHA before expensive work. That made a reproducible old build look like a failed test of new code.
- **Fix applied**: The artifact target now requires `worktree-guard` before provisioning or building anything, recursively enters the builder only after that proof, resolves `HEAD` once, prints `LINUX_BINARY_SOURCE sha=<full SHA>`, and archives that exact value. A dirty tree fails before Lima, Buildx, dependency sync, or PyInstaller.
- **Evidence**: A failing-first structural regression required the clean-tree dependency, exact-SHA banner, and immutable archive operand. The dirty-tree replay stopped in 0.3 seconds at `WORKTREE-DIRTY`. Clean exact commit `084a591e5da69bbfeda3314ad4bfdbfbce09c835` then built successfully, printed the identical source SHA, produced a valid aarch64 ELF, and passed the exact 1,329-edge warning-graph audit.
- **Practitioner evidence**: The long-lived Stack Overflow discussions [“Git archive of repository with uncommitted changes”](https://stackoverflow.com/questions/2766600/git-archive-of-repository-with-uncommitted-changes) and [“Create git archive from repo without a commit”](https://stackoverflow.com/questions/55977761/create-git-archive-from-repo-without-a-commit/55977933) document that `git archive HEAD` excludes uncommitted work. Docker Buildx issue [#3917](https://github.com/docker/buildx/issues/3917) separately asks for explicit local-ref build semantics so committed-tree and working-tree inputs cannot be confused.
- **Lesson**: A reproducible build must both reject ambiguous working state and state the immutable source it consumed. Never infer that a command tested current edits merely because it ran from their directory.

### 2026-09-29 — (resolved locally; exact-candidate gate required) Pinned UV image carried the wrong Python patch

- **What happened**: After provisioning the missing Lima engine, the real Linux artifact replay proved that the digest-pinned `python3.12-bookworm-slim` UV image contained Python 3.12.12, not the required 3.12.14. The build rejected it before PyInstaller analysis.
- **Root cause**: The image digest made one artifact immutable, but the image's minor-only tag had been mistaken for an exact interpreter contract. Python and UV identity were coupled to a third party's image refresh schedule.
- **Fix applied**: A project-owned cached builder now composes the official Python 3.12.14 Bookworm image and UV 0.12.19 distroless image, each by immutable multi-architecture digest. The Dockerfile asserts both versions while building, and the Make owner re-runs the identity proof before artifact analysis.
- **Evidence**: The real failed replay reported `Expected Python 3.12.14 ... found 3.12.12`.
